A grown-up-sized long-sleeved, ankle-length, drop-hood zip-front onesie, with a deep attached hood, with attached gloves sewn into the sleeves, and footed with built-in soles, in bamboo-blend jersey, a real plain stretch suit and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a seamless photoreal digital print with true-to-life colours, designed to make the onesie look like the outfit of a dragon rider in a feminine-styled version. The whole suit is the outfit, front, back, arms and legs (and any hood, gloves or feet) are printed as the matching parts of it, with the seams and the zip hidden in the pattern. The print shows a hardened leather flight jacket with scale pads, trousers, with a goggled helm printed on the hood, boots printed on the feet, and printed gloves on the attached gloves at the sleeve ends. Turn it round and the back carries the rear of the outfit, with nothing left plain. The outfit's own cloth reaches every seam, cuff and hem with no border or backdrop. The print is applied to the suit's own pattern pieces, front, back, each sleeve and each leg are fully covered in the matching cloth of the outfit, with no leftover background colour between them. Where the outfit leaves arms or legs bare, the suit itself is cut sleeveless, short-sleeved or short-legged at that point, so the wearer's own limbs show; no printed arm, leg, hand, foot or skin appears on the suit's fabric. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The print is cut exactly to the suit, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The outline is exactly a onesie's, nothing sticks out past the suit's edges, and no coat tails, skirts, capes, extra sleeves or loose layers hang outside it. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided suit shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. The hood carries only hood cloth, no face, hair, ears or head is printed on it, and no crowd, audience, stage, lights or scene appears anywhere on the suit, only the outfit's own cloth. The printed top of trousers reaching the hem and ending there. Raised plush layers are sewn on over the print, the medals and ribbons in velvet, the epaulettes in felt and the badges and patches in chenille, standing out from the surface. Printed and plush together, the outfit is printed all over, with padded appliqué parts standing out from the surface. A full dragon rider in a feminine-styled version costume, head to toe in a single suit, with a hardened leather flight jacket with scale pads, trousers on the legs, a goggled helm (as the hood), boots (as the feet), a saddle strap sewn or attached to the suit. Nothing of the outfit is missing and the legs of the suit show the lower garment. Novelty embellishments, rhinestone buttons down the front, hand-stitched patchwork appliqué in bright colours, knitted rib panels with raised cable texture and braided cord drawstrings with knotted ends. It reads as a joyful, exaggerated novelty onesie, stuffed with texture and detail.

The outline is exactly a onesie's, nothing sticks out past the suit's edges, and no coat tails, skirts, capes, extra sleeves or loose layers hang outside it. The outfit is laid out on the suit's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. It is not a drawing of a person standing in the middle of the suit, no body, no head, no limbs and no figure are pictured, only the outfit's cloth, trims and fastenings spread over the whole suit. Raised plush layers are sewn on over the print, the cuffs in fleece, the straps and holsters in faux fur and the epaulettes in faux leather, standing out from the surface. The print gives the whole outfit, and raised layers of soft fabric are sewn on over it for the collar, pockets, belt, buttons and badges, so the suit is half picture and half sculpture.
